# zk.de forum: high-engagement topic sweep

Ticket: https://github.com/theaustinschool/game-design-map/issues/8
Scope: non-staff posts in the most-replied forum.zettelkasten.de threads on atomicity, titles, links and link context, structure notes and hubs, IDs and Folgezettel, note length, own words, literature notes, and good-zettel examples.
Swept: 2026-11-04

| 08-860 | Notes for fiction writing (story arc, character notes, descriptions, maps) do not correspond to knowledge building blocks. | Not one of those things corresponds to any knowledge building block so far as I can see. | https://forum.zettelkasten.de/discussion/comment/25376#Comment_25376 | tomp | 2026-06-06 | atomicity,other:fiction | Vanilla API v2 2026-11-04 |

| 08-862 | Knowledge building blocks are best understood as conceptual structures viable for a given purpose (science, fiction, etc.) (citing von Glasersfeld). | it can mean instead that the building blocks are conceptual structures that are viable for a given purpose | https://forum.zettelkasten.de/discussion/comment/25385#Comment_25385 | Andy | 2026-06-09 | atomicity | Vanilla API v2 2026-11-04 |
| 08-863 | What counts as a viable note type is the owner's call, but it helps to learn from and adapt the structures others found viable for the same purpose. | if the purpose is one that other "epistemic agents" have shared in the past, it may help to learn from and adapt their knowledge | https://forum.zettelkasten.de/discussion/comment/25385#Comment_25385 | Andy | 2026-06-09 | atomicity | Vanilla API v2 2026-11-04 |
| 08-864 | Deeper practice means using more, and more systematic, viable conceptual structures for your purposes. | The deeper levels of the iceberg model are about having and using more, and more systematic, viable conceptual structures | https://forum.zettelkasten.de/discussion/comment/25385#Comment_25385 | Andy | 2026-06-09 | atomicity | Vanilla API v2 2026-11-04 |

| 08-876 | Put your own thoughts, doubts, questions, and observations into notes; a Zettelkasten is not only extracting others' concepts. | don't forget to involve your thoughts, your doubts, your questions, your observations, and so on into the stuff you'll process | https://forum.zettelkasten.de/discussion/comment/26342#Comment_26342 | andang76 | 2026-09-02 | own-words | Vanilla API v2 2026-11-04 |

| 08-1008 | ID-only file names make graph views meaningless and stop you from searching for a note to link it. | you can’t search for a note in order to link it (unless you have some insanely clever method of remembering each note’s unique ID) | https://forum.zettelkasten.de/discussion/comment/26676#Comment_26676 | jemscott | 2026-10-21 | title,id,tooling | Vanilla API v2 2026-11-04 |
| 08-1009 | Each note should have a unique system ID plus at least one human-readable title or name. | an objects should have a unique ID, to be used by the system. | 08-1010 | Put the target's title next to its ID in a link so you can decide whether to follow it. | I usually want to see the title of a note being linked to, so I can decide if I want to go look at it or not. | https://forum.zettelkasten.de/discussion/comment/26677#Comment_26677 | tomp | 2026-10-21 | title,link-context | Vanilla API v2 2026-11-04 |
| 08-1011 | Searching by title keywords can cause tunnel vision and reduce serendipity; ID-only file names push you through links and structure notes instead. | keyword searching for the title often makes me a bit too tunnel-vision-y, and reduces the "fabrication of serendipity" a bit for me | https://forum.zettelkasten.de/discussion/comment/16341#Comment_16341 | oddlynx | 2022-08-26 | title,failure-mode | Vanilla API v2 2026-11-04 |
| 08-1012 | Give each zettel a good title and fold a short version of it into the file name. | I am a believer in having a good title for each Zettel, a short version of which gets incorporated into the filename | https://forum.zettelkasten.de/discussion/comment/16337#Comment_16337 | GeoEng51 | 2022-08-26 | title | Vanilla API v2 2026-11-04 |
| 08-1013 | Writing the title before the content can force the content in a direction and limit it. | Sometimes a problem with titles is that I tend to write them before writing the content, thereby forcing the content in a certain direction. | https://forum.zettelkasten.de/discussion/comment/16350#Comment_16350 | erikh | 2022-08-27 | title,process,failure-mode | Vanilla API v2 2026-11-04 |
| 08-1014 | As understanding grows and notes become more granular, old titles stop fitting and need renaming. | when understanding on a topic increases, and my notes become more granular, the original titles do not always make sense anymore | https://forum.zettelkasten.de/discussion/comment/16350#Comment_16350 | erikh | 2022-08-27 | title,review | Vanilla API v2 2026-11-04 |
| 08-1015 | With good linking and tagging the title matters little. | if your link and tag well, the title of a note does not matter | https://forum.zettelkasten.de/discussion/comment/16350#Comment_16350 | erikh | 2022-08-27 | title,linking,tags | Vanilla API v2 2026-11-04 |
| 08-1016 | Treat early titles as provisional reminders and update title and file name as the zettel matures. | I don't feel too restricted by either the title or filename - they are just suggestions or reminders for content, in the beginning, inviting further thought and maturation | https://forum.zettelkasten.de/discussion/comment/16365#Comment_16365 | GeoEng51 | 2022-08-28 | title,review | Vanilla API v2 2026-11-04 |
| 08-1017 | Tag each zettel with its maturity state (new, in process, getting there, mature) and periodically work on immature ones. | I have several tags that I use to indicate the state of the zettel (new, in process, getting there, mature). | https://forum.zettelkasten.de/discussion/comment/16365#Comment_16365 | GeoEng51 | 2022-08-28 | tags,review | Vanilla API v2 2026-11-04 |
| 08-1018 | Link as you create zettels, and regularly revisit poorly linked zettels to add links. | part of my ZK maintenance activity is to regularly check poorly linked zettels to see if new links have become apparent | https://forum.zettelkasten.de/discussion/comment/16365#Comment_16365 | GeoEng51 | 2022-08-28 | linking,review | Vanilla API v2 2026-11-04 |
| 08-1019 | A composite title that joins two linked ideas carries more meaning into a link than either part alone. | their advantage is that when you make a link using them as title you bring into the context of link more meaning than having only one of the two parts | https://forum.zettelkasten.de/discussion/comment/20577#Comment_20577 | andang76 | 2024-06-24 | title,link-context | Vanilla API v2 2026-11-04 |
| 08-1020 | Keep each argument in its own note, because each is open to criticism separately. | All your arguments are subject to criticism. | 08-1022 | Alongside atomic notes, use small "molecule" notes that link two atomic notes into a composite idea, without copying their content. | I think that a viable solution is having three notes, two atoms and a... molecule: | https://forum.zettelkasten.de/discussion/comment/20727#Comment_20727 | andang76 | 2024-07-10 | atomicity,structure-note | Vanilla API v2 2026-11-04 |

| 08-1025 | Not every note needs supporting argument and evidence; notes can serve non-epistemic ends like motivation. | a Zettelkasten can serve epistemic and non-epistemic ends | https://forum.zettelkasten.de/discussion/comment/20725#Comment_20725 | ZettelDistraction | 2024-07-10 | other:purpose | Vanilla API v2 2026-11-04 |

| 08-1028 | Scanning your top-level notes before placing a new note keeps its first link from being lazily obvious. | a habit of running through my top levels before I decide where to put the note helps keep me from lazily making the first link on the new note something obvious | https://forum.zettelkasten.de/discussion/comment/24082#Comment_24082 | marick | 2025-10-28 | folgezettel,linking | Vanilla API v2 2026-11-04 |
| 08-1029 | In digital systems an elaborate numbering scheme isn't needed because you can structure ideas on the fly. | With digital systems, it is every easy to structure sets of ideas on the fly, so an elaborate numbering system isn't needed. | https://forum.zettelkasten.de/discussion/comment/24128#Comment_24128 | Nick | 2025-11-05 | folgezettel | Vanilla API v2 2026-11-04 |
| 08-1030 | Folgezettel prefixes cluster notes around emergent topics, and prefix search aids serendipitous discovery. | folgezettels with the same prefix from a cluster of notes around an “emergent” topic that you didn’t explicitly attempt to create | https://forum.zettelkasten.de/discussion/comment/24142#Comment_24142 | zettelsan | 2025-11-06 | folgezettel | Vanilla API v2 2026-11-04 |

| 08-1082 | A zettel should have a single focus, and a link should mark a change of focus to another zettel (citing Bianca Pereira). | 1) Zettels should have a single focus, and 2) links should indicate a change of focus | https://forum.zettelkasten.de/discussion/comment/19649#Comment_19649 | ZettelDistraction | 2024-02-19 | atomicity,linking | Vanilla API v2 2026-11-04 |

| 08-1086 | Use three note types: single-focus zettels, structure notes, and index notes. | The three types are single-focus Zettel (Dan Allosso calls these "Point Notes"), Structure Notes, and Index Notes. | https://forum.zettelkasten.de/discussion/comment/19708#Comment_19708 | ZettelDistraction | 2024-02-26 | structure-note,hub | Vanilla API v2 2026-11-04 |
| 08-1087 | Annotate wikilinks in the body where they add to the topic's context; index-note links need only the title. | Annotate WikiLinks where they contribute to the topic's context, except for Index Note references. | https://forum.zettelkasten.de/discussion/comment/19708#Comment_19708 | ZettelDistraction | 2024-02-26 | link-context | Vanilla API v2 2026-11-04 |
| 08-1088 | Write zettels for your future self, not for a general readership. | the advice to write for your future self, not for a general readership | https://forum.zettelkasten.de/discussion/comment/19710#Comment_19710 | ZettelDistraction | 2024-02-26 | audience | Vanilla API v2 2026-11-04 |
| 08-1089 | Put links to index notes and distantly related notes, plus hashtags, in a separate SEE ALSO section at the end, apart from annotated body links. | This section is for index notes and more distantly related notes | https://forum.zettelkasten.de/discussion/comment/19713#Comment_19713 | ZettelDistraction | 2024-02-27 | linking,other:layout | Vanilla API v2 2026-11-04 |
| 08-1090 | Single focus works like focus in photography: the main subject stays sharp while relevant peripheral elements may be present without detracting from it. | Apply the concept of focus in Single-focus Zettels akin to focus in photography: ensure the main subject remains sharply defined while peripheral elements, present but secondary, complement rather than detract from the central theme. | https://forum.zettelkasten.de/discussion/comment/19723#Comment_19723 | ZettelDistraction | 2024-02-27 | atomicity | Vanilla API v2 2026-11-04 |
| 08-1091 | Place each link as close as possible to the idea it is associated with. | The rule I use is 'put the link as close to the idea the link is associated with.' | https://forum.zettelkasten.de/discussion/comment/19738#Comment_19738 | Will | 2024-02-28 | linking | Vanilla API v2 2026-11-04 |

| 08-1499 | Folgezettel IDs act like a personal trainer: needing to relate each note loosely to the next creates focus, even if not strictly necessary. | there is something about the id's and just needing to loosely relate one to the next one that helps to create focus | https://forum.zettelkasten.de/discussion/2739/i-am-been-testing-things-out-on-something-and-wanted-to-share | rwrobinson | 2023-11-25 | folgezettel,process | Vanilla API v2 2026-11-04 |
| 08-1500 | Don't use Folgezettel as categories; they are trains of thought (citing Doto). | I've been using wrongly the folgezettel (as categories, not trains of thought; I read "Folgezettel is not an Outline" by Bob Doto and understood it, finally). | https://forum.zettelkasten.de/discussion/comment/18928#Comment_18928 | F_Macías | 2023-11-26 | folgezettel | Vanilla API v2 2026-11-04 |
| 08-1501 | A UID gives a concept a permanent address, makes any zettel linkable, and lets you hang new cards onto any zettel (Anschlussfähigkeit). | The value of the UID is that it enables 3 things: | https://forum.zettelkasten.de/discussion/comment/19324#Comment_19324 | JasperMcFly | 2024-01-16 | id,folgezettel | Vanilla API v2 2026-11-04 |
| 08-1502 | Pre-established category numbers in UIDs are a bad idea; let structure emerge. Plain timestamps are enough. | I think the idea of preestablished categories, as with many of Scheper's (hot) takes, is a terrible idea. The joy of a Zettelkasten is to allow for emergence and roaming. | https://forum.zettelkasten.de/discussion/comment/19326#Comment_19326 | KillerWhale | 2024-01-16 | id,folgezettel,failure-mode | Vanilla API v2 2026-11-04 |
| 08-1503 | Encoding a subject heading (or much other metadata) in the UID is generally inadvisable. | I think encoding a subject heading in the UID is generally inadvisable for all the reasons already stated | https://forum.zettelkasten.de/discussion/comment/19336#Comment_19336 | Andy | 2024-01-16 | id | Vanilla API v2 2026-11-04 |
| 08-1504 | With Luhmann-style numbering, start each unconnected topic at a new number and let topics emerge within topics; index where they appear rather than pre-defining categories. | It is not a pre-defined category, it emerges naturally. I don't see the point why I should add pre-defined categories | https://forum.zettelkasten.de/discussion/comment/19338#Comment_19338 | ChristophPleininger | 2024-01-16 | folgezettel,id,hub | Vanilla API v2 2026-11-04 |
| 08-1505 | Combine chronological numbering (yymmdd-nn) with Folgezettel-style extensions when a new note continues an existing one. | I started off with a time-stamping approach to numbering my notes, but have come to make use of 'Folgezettel' as well. | https://forum.zettelkasten.de/discussion/comment/10902#Comment_10902 | EconomistMike | 2021-03-08 | id,folgezettel | Vanilla API v2 2026-11-04 |
| 08-1506 | Use shorter IDs (e.g. YYMNNN) because long 12-digit timestamps crowd out titles in file lists and carry redundancy. | I have been using this format, but I find it hard to scroll through my files in the Finder. | https://forum.zettelkasten.de/discussion/598/zettel-id-format | JackMcI | 2019-05-19 | id,title | Vanilla API v2 2026-11-04 |
| 08-1507 | Mix a descriptive part with a UID part in IDs instead of long timestamps; it also allows Folgezettel and proximity connections. | my IDs are a mixture of a descriptive part and UID part that can include a date or initials of the referenced ressource. | https://forum.zettelkasten.de/discussion/comment/2913#Comment_2913 | gescho | 2019-05-25 | id,folgezettel | Vanilla API v2 2026-11-04 |
| 08-1508 | Note IDs are permalinks; sequential numbers suit paper, but digital systems risk collisions when syncing, hence timestamps. | Note IDs are like permalinks. Having a stamped identifier that doesn't change means you can find it universally. | https://forum.zettelkasten.de/discussion/comment/2919#Comment_2919 | JustinW80 | 2019-05-28 | id | Vanilla API v2 2026-11-04 |
| 08-1509 | Zettel IDs are used outside the note tool too, so pick an ID that works on paper and across tools. | my Zettel IDs also have much use outside of the tool in which the Zettels are written, forming a link between them. | https://forum.zettelkasten.de/discussion/1037/my-choice-for-the-zettel-id | grayen | 2020-04-16 | id | Vanilla API v2 2026-11-04 |
| 08-1510 | Timestamp IDs can be generated by hand without a machine; base32-encoded millisecond timestamps keep automated IDs short. | timestamp-based IDs have the HUGE advantage that they can be generated manually, w/o the need for a machine. | https://forum.zettelkasten.de/discussion/comment/5410#Comment_5410 | msteffens | 2020-04-17 | id,tooling | Vanilla API v2 2026-11-04 |
| 08-1511 | Extend the Zettel ID idea to a general unique identifier for any object (books, documents, to-dos, events) so you can reference across media. | In my personal system, any object can have a GUID (General Unique Identifier) - Zettels, books, paper documents, even To-Dos or (digital/analog) calendar events - your imagination is the limit. | https://forum.zettelkasten.de/discussion/613/zettel-id-and-general-unique-identifiers | gescho | 2019-05-25 | id | Vanilla API v2 2026-11-04 |
| 08-1512 | Embedding contextual information in a UID runs into "The Filing Problem": you must know in advance what the thing is going to be. | I feel like for me this would fall prey to what I call The Filing Problem | https://forum.zettelkasten.de/discussion/comment/2916#Comment_2916 | mediapathic | 2019-05-26 | id,failure-mode | Vanilla API v2 2026-11-04 |
| 08-1513 | Put the Zettel ID at the end of the file name so the note list reads in a friendlier, more browsable way. | For the same reason, I initially started with my Zettel IDs at the end of the filename, like /FileName-20180403155000. | https://forum.zettelkasten.de/discussion/comment/1203#Comment_1203 | rrieke | 2018-04-03 | id,title | Vanilla API v2 2026-11-04 |
| 08-1514 | Include seconds in timestamp IDs so two notes never get the same ID. | ok, so the best way to handle this is to have seconds included, since it is almost impossible to create multiple notes in the same second. | https://forum.zettelkasten.de/discussion/comment/1842#Comment_1842 | tonari | 2018-10-04 | id | Vanilla API v2 2026-11-04 |
| 08-1515 | A literature note is a bibliographic reference in a reference manager with attached notes; it never goes into the slip-box, only Zettels do (citing Ahrens). | A literature note is a bibliographic reference in a reference manager, with one or more attached notes. | https://forum.zettelkasten.de/discussion/comment/14269#Comment_14269 | ZettelDistraction | 2022-02-06 | literature-note,sources | Vanilla API v2 2026-11-04 |
| 08-1516 | Literature notes are not moved into the main Zettelkasten; new permanent notes are written in the main Zettelkasten based on them. | What is really happening is that new permanent notes are created in the main Zettelkasten based on the literature notes from the bibliographic Zettelkasten. | https://forum.zettelkasten.de/discussion/comment/14180#Comment_14180 | zk_1000 | 2022-02-02 | literature-note,process | Vanilla API v2 2026-11-04 |
| 08-1517 | A separate "literature note" type is unnecessary; one can work without it. | Personally, I don't even use the term or have use for such a note. | https://forum.zettelkasten.de/discussion/comment/14189#Comment_14189 | taurusnoises | 2022-02-02 | literature-note | Vanilla API v2 2026-11-04 |
| 08-1518 | Revise permanent notes with caution: edits can strip a self-contained note of context, ripple to linked notes, and erase the record of mistakes (citing Ahrens). | They can be revised—however, I advise caution when revising permanent notes. | https://forum.zettelkasten.de/discussion/comment/14269#Comment_14269 | ZettelDistraction | 2022-02-06 | review,evergreen | Vanilla API v2 2026-11-04 |
| 08-1519 | Every note passes through phases; a zettel should never settle into a static form. | every note goes through phases, and in some sense, a zettel should never settle into a static persona. | https://forum.zettelkasten.de/discussion/comment/14184#Comment_14184 | Will | 2022-02-02 | evergreen | Vanilla API v2 2026-11-04 |
| 08-1520 | Zettels are written in the writer's own words (citing Ahrens). | I should have mentioned that Zettels are written in the writer's own words. | https://forum.zettelkasten.de/discussion/comment/14379#Comment_14379 | ZettelDistraction | 2022-02-11 | own-words | Vanilla API v2 2026-11-04 |
| 08-1521 | Literature notes hold the source's view (quotes plus a restatement of the authors' points); zettels hold your own point of view on what you internalized. | For me the main difference between literature notes and zettels is the point of view. Literature are reference that point to "other" knowledge, zettels are "my knowledge" | https://forum.zettelkasten.de/discussion/comment/17036#Comment_17036 | andang76 | 2022-12-15 | literature-note,own-words | Vanilla API v2 2026-11-04 |
| 08-1522 | Skip the literature-note step: refactor source notes and highlights directly into autonomous atomic zettels until nothing of the original is left. | Each zettel consumes part of the original until none is left; then, I know I'm done. | https://forum.zettelkasten.de/discussion/comment/17037#Comment_17037 | Will | 2022-12-16 | literature-note,process,atomicity | Vanilla API v2 2026-11-04 |
| 08-1523 | When a source note is refactored into zettels, each zettel takes on your personal point of view while keeping the supporting references. | When refactored, each note fills with the personal POV and, significantly, the supporting references from the initial go around. | https://forum.zettelkasten.de/discussion/comment/17037#Comment_17037 | Will | 2022-12-16 | own-words,sources | Vanilla API v2 2026-11-04 |
| 08-1524 | An intermediate literature note is worth it only when the source is long, complex, or important enough to need staged processing. | I think that literature notes (my version of literature note, at least), using a source note, are relevant if the content I process requires intermediate steps, because of its size, its complexity or its' importants. | https://forum.zettelkasten.de/discussion/comment/17042#Comment_17042 | andang76 | 2022-12-16 | literature-note,process | Vanilla API v2 2026-11-04 |
| 08-1525 | The "literature note" concept is not operational in digital practice; whatever ends up in the reference manager is the literature note, so the term may not be worth keeping. | I'm not convinced that retaining the notion of "literature note" is so useful. | https://forum.zettelkasten.de/discussion/comment/16364#Comment_16364 | ZettelDistraction | 2022-08-28 | literature-note | Vanilla API v2 2026-11-04 |
| 08-1526 | To write in your own words, take brief notes while reading, let them sit a day or two, then write the zettel without looking at the original so you must recall and rethink the idea. | write that zettel without reference to the original material. | https://forum.zettelkasten.de/discussion/comment/13136#Comment_13136 | GeoEng51 | 2021-10-03 | own-words,process | Vanilla API v2 2026-11-04 |
| 08-1527 | You need some way to distance yourself from the original text so it does not become a crutch when you write the idea down. | I believe you need some way to distance yourself from the original text, so that you don't use it as a crutch | https://forum.zettelkasten.de/discussion/comment/13136#Comment_13136 | GeoEng51 | 2021-10-03 | own-words | Vanilla API v2 2026-11-04 |
| 08-1528 | Write the note as if telling or teaching a friend what you learned; you can't recall exact phrasing, so it comes out in your own terms. | I also try to imagine that I'm trying to tell/teach a friend what I learned from a reference. | https://forum.zettelkasten.de/discussion/comment/13140#Comment_13140 | prometheanhindsight | 2021-10-04 | own-words,audience | Vanilla API v2 2026-11-04 |
| 08-1529 | "Own words" does not mean rewording a passage by swapping a few words; it means examining the idea against your own thinking and expanding, challenging, or explaining it. | To "rewrite in your own words" is to take the quote and first examine it in the context of your own thoughts on the subject. | https://forum.zettelkasten.de/discussion/comment/13146#Comment_13146 | taurusnoises | 2021-10-04 | own-words | Vanilla API v2 2026-11-04 |
| 08-1530 | If the author said it best and you have nothing to add, quote them directly; there is no rule that you must change their words. | If they said it best, and you have literally nothing to add, quote them directly. | https://forum.zettelkasten.de/discussion/comment/13146#Comment_13146 | taurusnoises | 2021-10-04 | own-words,sources | Vanilla API v2 2026-11-04 |
| 08-1531 | Always cite the source even after rewriting in your own words; restating an idea does not make it yours, because citations are for ideas, not strings of words. | ALWAYS cite your sources. Writing a quote in your own words does not make the idea your own. | https://forum.zettelkasten.de/discussion/comment/13146#Comment_13146 | taurusnoises | 2021-10-04 | sources,own-words | Vanilla API v2 2026-11-04 |
| 08-1532 | Letting a group of ideas stew over time (thinking, walking, talking it over) distills them, so when you write the zettel the thought is atomic and clearly defined. | So when I finally sit down to write, the thought is truly atomic and clearly defined. | https://forum.zettelkasten.de/discussion/comment/13156#Comment_13156 | GeoEng51 | 2021-10-04 | process,atomicity | Vanilla API v2 2026-11-04 |
| 08-1533 | Be selective: many ideas appeal at first only because they are shiny and new, not because they are deep or worthy, and sorting that out takes time. | Most of those initially exciting and interesting things appeal because they are shiny and new, but not necessarily because they are deep or profound or just simply worthy. | https://forum.zettelkasten.de/discussion/comment/13156#Comment_13156 | GeoEng51 | 2021-10-04 | process,failure-mode | Vanilla API v2 2026-11-04 |
| 08-1534 | Writing in your own words means adding to the author's statements based on your purpose for reading, not paraphrasing them without adding anything. | It means to add more to their statements based on your purpose for reading the source. | https://forum.zettelkasten.de/discussion/comment/13175#Comment_13175 | Annabella | 2021-10-06 | own-words | Vanilla API v2 2026-11-04 |
| 08-1535 | In reading notes, paraphrase rather than transcribe, because paraphrasing improves later recall. | But, I'd recommend to paraphrase instead. It improves recall of the ideas, which will be helpful later on. | https://forum.zettelkasten.de/discussion/comment/13175#Comment_13175 | Annabella | 2021-10-06 | own-words,literature-note | Vanilla API v2 2026-11-04 |
| 08-1536 | After writing from memory, check for gaps against your reading notes, the source, other sources, or your Zettelkasten, and fill them in. | Notice gaps in your description, then go back to your reading notes, the source, or additional sources, including your Zettelkasten, until you can fill them in. | https://forum.zettelkasten.de/discussion/comment/13175#Comment_13175 | Annabella | 2021-10-06 | own-words,process | Vanilla API v2 2026-11-04 |
| 08-1537 | Never skip citing: if you quote or paraphrase without citing, you imply the idea is yours. | If you don't cite, you're implying that you came up with the ideas. | https://forum.zettelkasten.de/discussion/comment/13175#Comment_13175 | Annabella | 2021-10-06 | sources | Vanilla API v2 2026-11-04 |
| 08-1538 | Atomization helps with own words: compressing a larger idea into a few sentences forces the thinking that produces your own formulation. | Writing short, encapsulating a more extensive idea in a few sentences helps with the thinking process necessary for ideation. | https://forum.zettelkasten.de/discussion/comment/13177#Comment_13177 | Will | 2021-10-06 | atomicity,own-words | Vanilla API v2 2026-11-04 |
| 08-1539 | Zettels are proxies for ideas, so a link connects ideas, not notes. | When making links of any type, you are connecting ideas, not notes. | https://forum.zettelkasten.de/discussion/comment/13177#Comment_13177 | Will | 2021-10-06 | linking | Vanilla API v2 2026-11-04 |
| 08-1540 | When processing a captured quote, ask what its main ideas are (each a candidate zettel title), then whether it changes notes you already have; failing that, ask why you captured it. | Second, if all else fails, I ask, Why did I capture this quote? | https://forum.zettelkasten.de/discussion/comment/13207#Comment_13207 | Steve625 | 2021-10-08 | own-words,process | Vanilla API v2 2026-11-04 |
| 08-1541 | For hard concepts, draw a diagram of the author's internal logic; it makes room to add your own take and to explain it. | Diagrams help me understand the internal logic of what the original writer is saying and gives me room to add my own logical take. | https://forum.zettelkasten.de/discussion/comment/13231#Comment_13231 | Darren_McDonald | 2021-10-11 | own-words,other:diagrams | Vanilla API v2 2026-11-04 |
| 08-1542 | Your own words come from having a different use for the information than the source did; that difference lets you communicate it in your own voice. | Very often you will have not have the exact same use for a piece of information as did the source you learned from. | https://forum.zettelkasten.de/discussion/comment/15756#Comment_15756 | jellis | 2022-05-13 | own-words | Vanilla API v2 2026-11-04 |
| 08-1543 | Don't paste an LLM's answer into a new note; add your own thinking on top of anything an LLM yields. | You should add your organic thinking process on top of what you yield from LLMs. | https://forum.zettelkasten.de/discussion/3454/ai-augmented-zettelkasten | c4lvorias | 2026-04-12 | own-words,other:ai | Vanilla API v2 2026-11-04 |
| 08-1544 | Treat the Zettelkasten as a quality-controlled environment that holds only content from verifiable sources, not LLM output. | I think of my ZK as a quality controlled environment: only content from verifiable sources. | https://forum.zettelkasten.de/discussion/comment/25054#Comment_25054 | harr | 2026-04-12 | sources,other:ai | Vanilla API v2 2026-11-04 |
| 08-1545 | If LLM-generated content goes into the Zettelkasten at all, keep it clearly marked and separated from verified content (a callout, frontmatter field, or separate note type). | My solution to storing LLM generated content in the ZK was to keep it clearly separated. | https://forum.zettelkasten.de/discussion/comment/25062#Comment_25062 | harr | 2026-04-12 | sources,other:ai | Vanilla API v2 2026-11-04 |
| 08-1546 | Keep human thinking separate from machine-produced text in your notes. | I still like to keep human thinking separate from machine produced text. | https://forum.zettelkasten.de/discussion/comment/25073#Comment_25073 | harr | 2026-04-14 | own-words,other:ai | Vanilla API v2 2026-11-04 |
| 08-1547 | Work a source in layers: rough notes in a per-source note, chunk them into the author's ideas, refactor each into a standalone note on that author's single idea, then write your own evergreen notes linking to them. | refactor the important ideas into their own standalone notes capturing that author's single idea | https://forum.zettelkasten.de/discussion/comment/11211#Comment_11211 | davecan | 2021-03-24 | literature-note,process,own-words | Vanilla API v2 2026-11-04 |
| 08-1548 | Process a source into your own idea-focused outline rather than notes organized by chapter, and favor synthesis notes over source-specific notes. | When I process a source I try to build my own idea-focused outline from the work wherever possible, whereas previously I would take notes by chapter/etc which was less effective. | https://forum.zettelkasten.de/discussion/comment/11274#Comment_11274 | davecan | 2021-03-26 | sources,structure-note | Vanilla API v2 2026-11-04 |
| 08-1549 | Instead of writing out what the source says, write questions about the text and answer them in your head the next day, which forces recall and prompts ideas beyond the text. | Instead of writing out explicitly what is in the source text (f.x.), I will try to formulate one or more questions about the text for me to review. | https://forum.zettelkasten.de/discussion/comment/11327#Comment_11327 | henrikenggaard | 2021-03-29 | own-words,literature-note | Vanilla API v2 2026-11-04 |
| 08-1550 | Highlighting material and turning it straight into cards or notes is too easy; the value comes from formulating it into your own words. | IR is very good if you formulate the material into your own words. The problem is that its too easy to just highlight some material and turn it into a cloze deletion flashcard. | https://forum.zettelkasten.de/discussion/comment/11380#Comment_11380 | davecan | 2021-03-31 | own-words,failure-mode | Vanilla API v2 2026-11-04 |
| 08-1551 | Wait a day or two, then write the explanation as if for someone unfamiliar with the topic, filling in the gaps where it fails. | a day or two later I try to explain it via writing as if I were explaining it to someone who was unfamiliar with it. | https://forum.zettelkasten.de/discussion/comment/11924#Comment_11924 | zhanzh3ng | 2021-05-23 | own-words,audience | Vanilla API v2 2026-11-04 |
| 08-1552 | Rigid note categories and formats are not required; a note needs only a UID and some text, and the collection grows organically. | I just make notes, and as long as each one has a UID and some text in it, I don't really care about anything else. | https://forum.zettelkasten.de/discussion/comment/11249#Comment_11249 | MartinBB | 2021-03-26 | literature-note,other:note-types | Vanilla API v2 2026-11-04 |
| 08-1553 | Skip named note types: every note in the ZK is just a zettel with one idea, written concisely, connected to two or more other zettels. | each zettel should contain one idea, be written concisely and be connected to two or more other zettels | https://forum.zettelkasten.de/discussion/comment/11266#Comment_11266 | GeoEng51 | 2021-03-26 | literature-note,atomicity,linking | Vanilla API v2 2026-11-04 |
| 08-1554 | Reading notes are shorthand kept outside the Zettelkasten and follow none of the zettel rules; zettels are created from them later. | They don't go directly into my ZK; they are "outside" my zettel | https://forum.zettelkasten.de/discussion/comment/11266#Comment_11266 | GeoEng51 | 2021-03-26 | literature-note,process | Vanilla API v2 2026-11-04 |
| 08-1555 | A literature note captures one idea from one source in that source's terms; a permanent note synthesizes material from sources into your own words so it becomes your own idea. | The difference is that a lit note captures a single idea from a single source in the terms of that source, while a permanent note is my effort to synthesize the material from the source(s) into my own words | https://forum.zettelkasten.de/discussion/comment/11284#Comment_11284 | davecan | 2021-03-27 | literature-note,own-words,atomicity | Vanilla API v2 2026-11-04 |
| 08-1556 | When onboarding a book, build your own preface and table of contents for it as a structure note populated with the ideas that caught your attention. | I get joy from creating my own preface to the book and my own index/table of contents | https://forum.zettelkasten.de/discussion/comment/11244#Comment_11244 | Will | 2021-03-26 | structure-note,sources | Vanilla API v2 2026-11-04 |
| 08-1557 | Distinguish source notes (other people's ideas written in your own words) from point notes (your own ideas connected to the ideas of others) (citing Allosso). | They are other people's ideas written down in my own words. Allosso call them Source Notes. Point Notes now are my own ideas connected to the ideas of others. | https://forum.zettelkasten.de/discussion/3120/keeping-own-ideas-in-zettelkasten | Edmund | 2024-12-12 | own-words,literature-note | Vanilla API v2 2026-11-04 |
| 08-1558 | A point note can be a new idea formed by applying the concept in one source note to the concept in another. | But they should show two independent ideas leading to a new one. | https://forum.zettelkasten.de/discussion/comment/22224#Comment_22224 | Edmund | 2024-12-12 | own-words,linking | Vanilla API v2 2026-11-04 |
| 08-1559 | You own an idea not by inventing it but by selecting it, decontextualizing and recontextualizing it, reframing it with your own point of view, and connecting it to your other thoughts. | is perfectly "owned" by me, perhaps with more or less different degrees but owned | https://forum.zettelkasten.de/discussion/comment/22226#Comment_22226 | andang76 | 2024-12-12 | own-words | Vanilla API v2 2026-11-04 |
| 08-1560 | Test what you consider your own ideas against more expert sources; ideas that survive scrutiny can grow. | Importantly, I want to test what I consider my ideas against the ideas of others who are more expert than myself to see how my ideas stand up. | https://forum.zettelkasten.de/discussion/comment/22231#Comment_22231 | Will | 2024-12-13 | own-words,sources | Vanilla API v2 2026-11-04 |
| 08-1561 | You make others' ideas your own by reframing and recontextualizing them, always with credit given. | there's a process whereby we work with the ideas of others and make them "our own" by reframing them and recontextualizing them. Always with appropriate credit given, of course. | https://forum.zettelkasten.de/discussion/comment/22238#Comment_22238 | DanAllosso | 2024-12-13 | own-words,sources | Vanilla API v2 2026-11-04 |
| 08-1562 | In historical research, primary-source evidence must be noted verbatim and kept complete so it can be quoted, rather than only summarized in your own words. | This usually involves noting the evidence verbatim. I may summarise it later, but I need to work from the original evidence complete and to quote it if necessary. | https://forum.zettelkasten.de/discussion/1873/zettelkasten-for-historical-research | pgrhowarth | 2021-06-20 | sources,own-words,other:history | Vanilla API v2 2026-11-04 |
| 08-1563 | Besides excerpts there are two note kinds: reformulations (summaries in your own words) and reflections (your own interpretations and reasoning); the Zettelkasten pays off when you add reflections (citing Luhmann). | The other note types (besides excerpts) are “reformulations” (as Luhmann referred to them) which are summaries in your own words. And “reflections” which add your own interpretations and reasonings. | https://forum.zettelkasten.de/discussion/comment/14483#Comment_14483 | scottscheper | 2022-02-18 | own-words,literature-note | Vanilla API v2 2026-11-04 |
| 08-1564 | Organize thoughts around ideas rather than around book titles. | The Zettelkasten is valuable for organizing your thoughts around ideas (instead of organized around book title) | https://forum.zettelkasten.de/discussion/comment/14483#Comment_14483 | scottscheper | 2022-02-18 | structure-note,sources | Vanilla API v2 2026-11-04 |
| 08-1565 | To move beyond copying others' ideas, transform them (create variations) and combine them into new ones. | Transform (create variations on the idea) | https://forum.zettelkasten.de/discussion/comment/12095#Comment_12095 | Nick | 2021-06-04 | own-words,process | Vanilla API v2 2026-11-04 |
| 08-1566 | What separates a Zettelkasten from collecting is that each zettel states an idea as succinctly as possible in your own words and is connected to other zettels. | You have stated ideas as succinctly as possible, in your own words, in each zettel. | https://forum.zettelkasten.de/discussion/comment/12101#Comment_12101 | GeoEng51 | 2021-06-04 | own-words,linking,failure-mode | Vanilla API v2 2026-11-04 |
| 08-1567 | Start from zettels holding your own thoughts and questions on topics you care about, and choose literature from there, rather than letting reading drive the work. | My own approach is to focus on topics I really care about, write zettels with my thoughts and questions and try to dig deeper into them. | https://forum.zettelkasten.de/discussion/comment/12130#Comment_12130 | Removed202201121 | 2021-06-06 | sources,process | Vanilla API v2 2026-11-04 |
| 08-1568 | Collecting other people's ideas is not bad when starting a new subject; you need background before you can judge importance or have your own thoughts. | I would like to play Devil's advocate and suggest that 'collecting' is not necessarily a bad thing, especially as you start on something new. | https://forum.zettelkasten.de/discussion/comment/12666#Comment_12666 | pgrhowarth | 2021-08-23 | sources,failure-mode | Vanilla API v2 2026-11-04 |
| 08-1569 | Research notes are better generated from an original note that occurs to you than from obligatory reading in hope of original work. | more research/reading based notes in my ZK are generated by first an "original" note | https://forum.zettelkasten.de/discussion/comment/12787#Comment_12787 | bradfordfournier | 2021-09-05 | sources,process | Vanilla API v2 2026-11-04 |
| 08-1570 | Place references so it is obvious what is referenced, ideally inline and as close to the cited text as possible, so you can find the exact passage later. | Personally, I reference inline and as close to the text as possible | https://forum.zettelkasten.de/discussion/comment/5513#Comment_5513 | henrikenggaard | 2020-04-19 | sources | Vanilla API v2 2026-11-04 |
| 08-1571 | Quotes may live in the Zettelkasten, but they must be clearly marked as quotes with accurate references, or you later cannot tell your words from the source's and risk plagiarism. | It's not that you can't have quotes in your Zettelkasten, it is just that they need to be clearly marked and have accurate references. | https://forum.zettelkasten.de/discussion/comment/5525#Comment_5525 | Will | 2020-04-19 | sources,own-words,failure-mode | Vanilla API v2 2026-11-04 |
| 08-1572 | A reference only needs enough information to find the source again and reach the exact page the note was built from. | I only make sure that a reference has enough pertinent information that I can find the source again and reach the exact page I used for creating my note. | https://forum.zettelkasten.de/discussion/comment/5543#Comment_5543 | sbicknel | 2020-04-20 | sources | Vanilla API v2 2026-11-04 |
| 08-1573 | Quote directly only when the author said it better than you could; otherwise summarize in your own thoughts, which proves you understood the passage and shows why it was worth noting. | I prefer to write my own thoughts to summarize a passage because it proves to me that I understand what was written and makes it more clear why the passage was worth noting. | https://forum.zettelkasten.de/discussion/comment/5950#Comment_5950 | sbicknel | 2020-05-07 | own-words,sources | Vanilla API v2 2026-11-04 |
| 08-1574 | Lecture notes (cues, facts, summary) remain fleeting notes no matter how often they are revised; they are not zettels. | No matter how many revisions these are still fleeting notes for me. | https://forum.zettelkasten.de/discussion/comment/11752#Comment_11752 | mmynsted | 2021-05-04 | literature-note,process | Vanilla API v2 2026-11-04 |
| 08-1575 | Linking literature notes together is not enough; you also need to write permanent notes that synthesize them. | I need to be creating "permanent notes;" up to now I have just been linking my lit. notes together. | https://forum.zettelkasten.de/discussion/comment/24486#Comment_24486 | zosimawormphd | 2026-01-21 | literature-note,failure-mode | Vanilla API v2 2026-11-04 |
| 08-1576 | Amassing a big collection of quotes from textbooks and relying on full-text search, without processing them, leaves the Zettelkasten unproductive. | had just amassed a huge collection of quotes from my school textbooks. | https://forum.zettelkasten.de/discussion/comment/24488#Comment_24488 | zosimawormphd | 2026-01-21 | literature-note,failure-mode | Vanilla API v2 2026-11-04 |
| 08-1577 | Use one note type: a pithy description of one idea in your own words, optionally a few selective quotes showing how others expressed it, links, and source descriptions; extra note types are distractions. | This description is written in my own language, i.e., I write down my understanding of the idea. | https://forum.zettelkasten.de/discussion/comment/24492#Comment_24492 | GeoEng51 | 2026-01-21 | own-words,sources,literature-note | Vanilla API v2 2026-11-04 |
| 08-1578 | Keep one note per book holding bibliographic data, longer quotations, and all your annotations while reading; reserve zettels for your own ideas and thoughts. | Rule of thumb: one book = one note. | https://forum.zettelkasten.de/discussion/comment/24495#Comment_24495 | harr | 2026-01-22 | literature-note,sources | Vanilla API v2 2026-11-04 |
| 08-1579 | If only one phrase in a source is useful, put it in that book's note rather than creating a separate source note for it. | If in doubt, write the phrase in your book note that contains everything about the book that contains the five pages. | https://forum.zettelkasten.de/discussion/comment/24495#Comment_24495 | harr | 2026-01-22 | literature-note | Vanilla API v2 2026-11-04 |
| 08-1580 | A Zettelkasten is a network of interconnected atomic ideas in your own words, unlike a commonplace book, which records other people's words as they originally appeared. | A Zettelkasten is a network of interconnected, atomic ideas in your own words. | https://forum.zettelkasten.de/discussion/1865/commonplace-and-zettelkasten | Sev_L | 2021-06-11 | own-words,atomicity,linking | Vanilla API v2 2026-11-04 |
| 08-1581 | A Zettelkasten can mix quotations, definitions, data, vocabulary, and examples of language alongside atomic zettels of your own ideas, with markers to identify each type. | my ZK also includes a miscellany of quotations, ideas, definitions, historical data, vocabulary words, examples of beautiful language, as well as plenty of 'true' atomic Zettels containing my own ideas | https://forum.zettelkasten.de/discussion/comment/12184#Comment_12184 | Phil | 2021-06-12 | sources,own-words | Vanilla API v2 2026-11-04 |
| 08-1582 | For quotes you admire, store the quote in the literature/bibliographic section with its source, and write zettels describing exactly what about it you like (reading like a writer), then link them. | use the lit/biblio section to store your selected quote or beautiful phrase, writing ect. with the source noted as usual | https://forum.zettelkasten.de/discussion/comment/12350#Comment_12350 | rachel | 2021-07-07 | literature-note,own-words,sources | Vanilla API v2 2026-11-04 |
| 08-1583 | Don't get hung up on note types; what matters is a note's connections to other notes and your ability to find them. | I wouldn't get hung up on note types. A note is a note. More important is its connection to other notes and your ability to find those connections. | https://forum.zettelkasten.de/discussion/comment/17867#Comment_17867 | Will | 2023-05-15 | literature-note,linking | Vanilla API v2 2026-11-04 |
| 08-1584 | Separate definition notes bloat the Zettelkasten; keep a single glossary where you paste the source definition and write an alternative in your own words above it. | I made the mistake of including "Definition notes", which bloated my ZK unfortunately. | https://forum.zettelkasten.de/discussion/comment/16773#Comment_16773 | Nick | 2022-11-12 | own-words,sources,failure-mode | Vanilla API v2 2026-11-04 |
| 08-1585 | In a glossary note, paste the source definition as an excerpt and write your own version of the definition above it. | So I have a single filed called glossary that I will paste a definition into as an excerpt then write my own version definition above the excerpt. | https://forum.zettelkasten.de/discussion/comment/16780#Comment_16780 | Nick | 2022-11-12 | own-words,sources | Vanilla API v2 2026-11-04 |
| 08-1586 | Where a quote goes depends on its role: a quote that supports an existing thought belongs inside that thought's zettel; a quote that inspires new thoughts becomes the starting zettel for them. | The quote "supports" a thought/Idea I had before. | https://forum.zettelkasten.de/discussion/comment/13816#Comment_13816 | myn_user | 2021-12-20 | sources,own-words | Vanilla API v2 2026-11-04 |
| 08-1587 | A quote worth keeping goes into a zettel verbatim, marked as a quote, with your own comments on why you like it or what it means, then linked like any other zettel; a separate quote-only zettel is busy work. | then it goes into a zettel verbatim, with the appropriate Markdown to show it is a quote, along with my own comments on why I like it and/or what I think it means. | https://forum.zettelkasten.de/discussion/comment/13820#Comment_13820 | GeoEng51 | 2021-12-20 | sources,own-words | Vanilla API v2 2026-11-04 |
| 08-1588 | When linking to a zettel, add a short description of the target that is specific to the current zettel's content, saying why the link is worth following. | This description is context sensitive to the content of the current zettel, to indicate why following that link might be of interest. | https://forum.zettelkasten.de/discussion/comment/13822#Comment_13822 | GeoEng51 | 2021-12-20 | link-context | Vanilla API v2 2026-11-04 |
| 08-1589 | Paraphrase when you can describe the idea more shortly and simply than the author; quote when the author's description is great, then elaborate and clarify it for yourself. | If I can make a shorter and simpler description than the author, I do so. | https://forum.zettelkasten.de/discussion/comment/13828#Comment_13828 | Annabella | 2021-12-21 | own-words,sources | Vanilla API v2 2026-11-04 |
| 08-1590 | Writing in your own words does not by itself make a permanent note; a note that still presents the author's view inside the source's context is a literature note even if paraphrased. | Sometimes I have the feeling of having still a content of a literature note even if I've already written the concept in my own words. | https://forum.zettelkasten.de/discussion/comment/21123#Comment_21123 | andang76 | 2024-08-25 | literature-note,own-words | Vanilla API v2 2026-11-04 |
| 08-1591 | Literature notes and permanent notes stay distinct: only links among permanent notes form the knowledge network; literature notes don't link to each other and are linked from permanent notes only as references. | Literature notes don't make the network of my knowledge, don't link together | https://forum.zettelkasten.de/discussion/comment/21137#Comment_21137 | andang76 | 2024-08-27 | literature-note,linking | Vanilla API v2 2026-11-04 |

| 08-1593 | Don't fret over naming note types: engage with the book any way you like, then pick only the best to write short, impactful notes placed next to related notes. | Step 2: Pick the best of the best to write short, impactful notes about things that will help you think or write. | https://forum.zettelkasten.de/discussion/comment/21136#Comment_21136 | JasperMcFly | 2024-08-27 | literature-note,process | Vanilla API v2 2026-11-04 |
| 08-1594 | A literature note should be written in your own words, use quotes very sparingly, correctly convey the author's idea, and carry an identifier plus enough bibliographic information to find the source (citing Ahrens). | The main content of the Literature Note should be written in your own words, using quotes very, very sparingly, and correctly convey the idea/concept of the author. | https://forum.zettelkasten.de/discussion/2879/literature-notes-vs-permanent-notes | VineDresser | 2024-04-22 | literature-note,own-words,sources | Vanilla API v2 2026-11-04 |
| 08-1595 | Literature notes need not be concise or in your own words; they can hold key phrases, excerpts, and length, as long as they prepare you to write a concise main note. | I don't feel lit notes have to be concise and in your own words | https://forum.zettelkasten.de/discussion/comment/20228#Comment_20228 | JasperMcFly | 2024-04-22 | literature-note,own-words | Vanilla API v2 2026-11-04 |
| 08-1596 | A permanent note need not be only your personal observations; it can be a concise reformulation or summary of decisive ideas from what you read. | Main permanent note does not always have to be just "your own personal observations", they can also be concise reformulations/summaries | https://forum.zettelkasten.de/discussion/comment/20228#Comment_20228 | JasperMcFly | 2024-04-22 | own-words,literature-note | Vanilla API v2 2026-11-04 |

| 08-1629 | Any note containing a quotation from a source is a literature note, even after you add your own comments to it. | Any note with a quotation from a literature source is a literature note. And even if we add our own comments within that note, it remains a literature note. | https://forum.zettelkasten.de/discussion/comment/26565#Comment_26565 | AvidReader | 2026-09-29 | literature-note,sources | Vanilla API v2 2026-11-04 |
| 08-1630 | Keep literature and personal notes together and separate them with keywords; gather definitions through a keyword or a "Definitions" hub note listing them. | You can use a "hub note" say titled "Definitions" with [[...]] listing of your notes containing definitions. | https://forum.zettelkasten.de/discussion/comment/26565#Comment_26565 | AvidReader | 2026-09-29 | literature-note,hub | Vanilla API v2 2026-11-04 |
| 08-1631 | Unlike progressive summarization, which preserves chunks of the original, the Zettelkasten approach throws away first-pass notes and stores only concepts reframed in your own words. | The Zettelkasten Method advocated on this site advises throwing away first pass notes, and only storing concepts reframed in your own words. | https://forum.zettelkasten.de/discussion/comment/1927#Comment_1927 | micahredding | 2018-11-03 | own-words,literature-note | Vanilla API v2 2026-11-04 |
| 08-1632 | Capture only the thoughts in a text that stand out to you, but capture more of their roots; most of the value comes from surprises later. | I think I lean towards only capturing the ones that stand out to me, but then trying to capture more of the "roots" of those. | https://forum.zettelkasten.de/discussion/comment/1932#Comment_1932 | micahredding | 2018-11-03 | sources,process | Vanilla API v2 2026-11-04 |
| 08-1633 | When you take an idea from a secondary citation, record it as such ("Baker 2018, citing Rugg 2002") and make a reference note for the unread original. | In the note for that information I can record the source as "Baker 2018, citing Rugg 2002." | https://forum.zettelkasten.de/discussion/1645/how-to-record-a-source-you-want-to-read-but-have-not-yet-read | Pelican | 2021-01-27 | sources,literature-note | Vanilla API v2 2026-11-04 |
| 08-1634 | Keep your to-read list outside the Zettelkasten so the ZK stays a space to work in, not a task list. | Personally, I try to keep my reading list separate from my ZK. | https://forum.zettelkasten.de/discussion/comment/10362#Comment_10362 | prometheanhindsight | 2021-01-27 | sources,other:reading-list | Vanilla API v2 2026-11-04 |
| 08-1635 | Put a reference section at the bottom of each main note listing its sources in order, mark the one that gave birth to the note, and point to entries from the text with numbers. | I' ve in my main (permanent) notes, at the bottom, a section called 'reference' in which I put an ordered list of links to the sources if needed. | https://forum.zettelkasten.de/discussion/comment/20222#Comment_20222 | andang76 | 2024-04-21 | sources | Vanilla API v2 2026-11-04 |
| 08-1636 | When a secondary source (e.g. a blog post) reports a study, go to the study itself and capture it rather than relying on the commentary. | I'd suggest going directly to the study and capturing it, skipping the blog post. | https://forum.zettelkasten.de/discussion/comment/20223#Comment_20223 | Will | 2024-04-21 | sources | Vanilla API v2 2026-11-04 |
| 08-1637 | Write notes so they keep their meaning even if the external sources disappear; capture important web content into your own system rather than relying on links. | write your notes with the mindset that they must "remain alive", maintain their mean, even if the sources totally disappear. | https://forum.zettelkasten.de/discussion/comment/20269#Comment_20269 | andang76 | 2024-04-24 | sources,own-words | Vanilla API v2 2026-11-04 |
| 08-1638 | When linking to an external source, add a textual description of the location within it (e.g. which section), since you often can't link to the exact paragraph. | In general, what I would do is add after the link a textual description of the location. | https://forum.zettelkasten.de/discussion/comment/20268#Comment_20268 | andang76 | 2024-04-24 | sources,link-context | Vanilla API v2 2026-11-04 |
| 08-1639 | Keep source material in a permanently retrievable form on your own computer rather than as links that can change. | The best source material is what you have saved in a "permanently" retrievable form on your own computer. | https://forum.zettelkasten.de/discussion/comment/20282#Comment_20282 | GeoEng51 | 2024-04-28 | sources | Vanilla API v2 2026-11-04 |
| 08-1640 | Give each source an ID and number its source notes from it (e.g. 0049-2), so a main note can point to a specific source note or page compactly. | I assign an ID number to each source, so for example, 0049 for Atomic Habits | https://forum.zettelkasten.de/discussion/comment/20235#Comment_20235 | JasperMcFly | 2024-04-23 | literature-note,sources,id | Vanilla API v2 2026-11-04 |
| 08-1641 | Keep one reference note per book containing a short list of references from its bibliography, and link to it from the permanent notes that draw on it. | Keep a "Reference Note" for your books. One book, one note. | https://forum.zettelkasten.de/discussion/comment/20974#Comment_20974 | Edmund | 2024-08-07 | literature-note,sources | Vanilla API v2 2026-11-04 |
| 08-1642 | Before reading a new book, create its reference note with a brief description and links to existing notes and to books in its bibliography you have read, forming a literature map. | To get an initial overview, I simply create a new Reference Note for the book in my Zettelkasten. It consists of a brief description of the book and a few references to existing notes. | https://forum.zettelkasten.de/discussion/2952/a-system-for-writing-literature-mapping | Edmund | 2024-07-31 | literature-note,linking | Vanilla API v2 2026-11-04 |
| 08-1643 | Link individual atomic statements from publications, with context or a link type such as "supports" or "rejects", rather than linking whole publications as citation indexes do. | With our Zettelkasten notes we’re one step further ahead as we’re creating links between individual (atomic) statements from these publications. | https://forum.zettelkasten.de/discussion/comment/20952#Comment_20952 | msteffens | 2024-08-05 | linking,link-context,sources | Vanilla API v2 2026-11-04 |
| 08-1644 | Treat all source content the same, chunked into one-idea notes; a book or paper gets only a summary note that is a thoughtful list of links to those notes with short abstracts and source links. | These notes are usually centered around a book or paper. | https://forum.zettelkasten.de/discussion/comment/2337#Comment_2337 | Will | 2019-02-01 | literature-note,structure-note | Vanilla API v2 2026-11-04 |
| 08-1645 | Most notes from a source should be your take on what the author wrote; only a few are short pithy quotes. | A couple of notes are just a short pithy quote. But most are like this, my take of what what the author wrote. | https://forum.zettelkasten.de/discussion/comment/2355#Comment_2355 | Will | 2019-02-03 | own-words,sources | Vanilla API v2 2026-11-04 |
| 08-1646 | Keep skimmed and highlighted sources outside the Zettelkasten and zettelize them fully only just-in-time, when a project needs them, rather than digesting every source completely. | Eventually, if I ever do use the material in a writing project, I'll fully zettelize it on a "just-in-time" basis. | https://forum.zettelkasten.de/discussion/comment/2519#Comment_2519 | Eurobubba | 2019-02-27 | literature-note,process | Vanilla API v2 2026-11-04 |
| 08-1647 | Keep highlighted but not fully processed sources in the system, separated from zettels written in your own voice. | right now it still seems useful to be able to keep highlighted but not yet fully abgeklärt PDFs in the system, with some separation from zettels written in my own voice | https://forum.zettelkasten.de/discussion/comment/2374#Comment_2374 | Eurobubba | 2019-02-06 | literature-note,own-words | Vanilla API v2 2026-11-04 |
| 08-1648 | You can't know in advance which part of a source will matter later, so judging relevance by deferring digestion doesn't work; that uncertainty is why you process into the Zettelkasten. | If I knew what part of which current document was key to future use, then I’d not need a Zettelkasten at all. | https://forum.zettelkasten.de/discussion/comment/2362#Comment_2362 | Will | 2019-02-04 | process,sources | Vanilla API v2 2026-11-04 |
| 08-1649 | Keep all literature notes, and link to them from theory notes as their evidence or data; they also record what you took away from a source. | If it suggested a theory, I'd link to the literature note as evidence/data for the theory. | https://forum.zettelkasten.de/discussion/comment/16299#Comment_16299 | JoshA | 2022-08-22 | literature-note,linking | Vanilla API v2 2026-11-04 |
| 08-1650 | Keep every source as its own literature note in the ZK (named Author_Year_Title) holding the reference, a link to the source, and reading notes; process only the currently relevant parts into permanent notes and leave the rest for later rereading. | every source (book, article) gets its own literature note, named as Author(s)_Year_Title) | https://forum.zettelkasten.de/discussion/comment/16300#Comment_16300 | erikh | 2022-08-22 | literature-note,sources | Vanilla API v2 2026-11-04 |
| 08-1651 | After dividing a literature note's ideas into atomic notes, move the literature note out of the slip-box into the reference manager so it is kept but not duplicated. | I cut and paste all the literature notes into Zotero, my bibliography repository. | https://forum.zettelkasten.de/discussion/comment/16310#Comment_16310 | cctrovall | 2022-08-23 | literature-note | Vanilla API v2 2026-11-04 |
| 08-1652 | Don't throw literature notes away too easily: in academic work you need to track which concept came from which source, and keeping them lets you revisit the source in depth later. | Hard experience has taught me not to throw away these things too easily. | https://forum.zettelkasten.de/discussion/comment/16330#Comment_16330 | erikh | 2022-08-26 | literature-note,sources | Vanilla API v2 2026-11-04 |
| 08-1653 | Keep literature notes, because they can become or inspire different zettels at different times. | Also, I strongly advise to keep literature notes. They may become or inspire different Zettels in different moments in time. | https://forum.zettelkasten.de/discussion/comment/19575#Comment_19575 | Anna_Eleuteri | 2024-02-11 | literature-note | Vanilla API v2 2026-11-04 |
| 08-1654 | Whether you need intermediate reading notes depends on how profound the source is relative to what you know: most sources yield a few ideas you can note directly, while hard ones need reading notes first. | For me, it seems to depend on the profundity of the source, relative to my existing knowledge on the subject. | https://forum.zettelkasten.de/discussion/comment/16405#Comment_16405 | erikh | 2022-09-05 | literature-note,process | Vanilla API v2 2026-11-04 |
| 08-1655 | Link literature notes from evergreen and hub notes; as literature notes on a topic accumulate, write evergreen notes on their common principles and repoint the hub to those, which in turn point to the literature notes. | As I accumulate more literature notes on a topic I may create more evergreen notes that encompass common principles and replace some of those hub/outline pointers to the literature notes with pointers to the new evergreen notes instead. | https://forum.zettelkasten.de/discussion/comment/11037#Comment_11037 | davecan | 2021-03-16 | literature-note,hub,evergreen | Vanilla API v2 2026-11-04 |
| 08-1656 | When creating a zettel, look for a hub note whose outline could use it and link it there; if none fits, start a hub with this zettel (citing Matuschak). | when I create a zettel, I search through my hub notes for an outline that could make use of this zettel. If I don’t find a hub that fits, I start one with this zettel. | https://forum.zettelkasten.de/discussion/comment/11029#Comment_11029 | Will | 2021-03-16 | hub,structure-note | Vanilla API v2 2026-11-04 |
| 08-1657 | First get clear on what a note is about (the person, the idea, the line of scholarship, the cited author); that decides how to write and link it and whether you need a separate source-citation note. | I think there is a core issue here, and that is: what is the subject of the note - why are you creating it and what is it about? | https://forum.zettelkasten.de/discussion/comment/25180#Comment_25180 | tomp | 2026-05-21 | literature-note,sources | Vanilla API v2 2026-11-04 |
| 08-1658 | Write brief "condensed reformulations" of the decisive ideas and conceptually significant terms in what you read, not the filler (citing Luhmann, Learning How to Read). | I interpret his advice to write "condensed reformulations" of what we read and to focus on terms with conceptual significance as a recommendation to write brief notes on these decisive ideas and concepts. | https://forum.zettelkasten.de/discussion/comment/25197#Comment_25197 | JasperMcFly | 2026-05-23 | own-words,literature-note | Vanilla API v2 2026-11-04 |
| 08-1659 | A raw buffer note on a source becomes a literature note once its ideas are reworked into your own reactions, and becomes permanent once massaged into atomic, conceptual notes that are not directly derivative but still reference the literature note. | Finally a literature note becomes ::permanent (and goes in the /zettelkasten folder) when I have massaged it (into one or more) atomic, conceptual, not directly derivative | https://forum.zettelkasten.de/discussion/1934/share-your-folders-and-note-types-here-are-mine | bradfordfournier | 2021-08-25 | literature-note,own-words,process | Vanilla API v2 2026-11-04 |
| 08-1660 | Note categories (literature, permanent, etc.) are training wheels that help beginners, to be dropped later in favor of a single folder of notes. | IMO categories help in the beginning to get an idea of what you should do. They're like the training wheels of a bicycle. | https://forum.zettelkasten.de/discussion/comment/12775#Comment_12775 | Annabella | 2021-09-03 | literature-note,other:note-types | Vanilla API v2 2026-11-04 |
| 08-1661 | Give literature notes phrase-based titles capturing the author's specific point, with the source's notes and quotes supporting it inside. | My approach is to give literature notes useful phrase-based titles whenever possible | https://forum.zettelkasten.de/discussion/comment/11002#Comment_11002 | davecan | 2021-03-14 | literature-note,title | Vanilla API v2 2026-11-04 |
| 08-1662 | Don't write a duplicate evergreen note that only restates a literature note; create one only when another source or your own insight expands the concept, and use it to synthesize. | In that case I do not create a "duplicate" evergreen note that only links to the literature note. | https://forum.zettelkasten.de/discussion/comment/11002#Comment_11002 | davecan | 2021-03-14 | literature-note,evergreen | Vanilla API v2 2026-11-04 |
| 08-1663 | Copy and paste of notes and highlights is fine as a preliminary step to note creation; remnants of it may stay or vanish as you reorganize and elaborate. | Cut and paste of the basic framework of notes and highlights is often the preliminary step to the actual note creation. | https://forum.zettelkasten.de/discussion/comment/11003#Comment_11003 | Will | 2021-03-14 | own-words,process | Vanilla API v2 2026-11-04 |
| 08-1664 | Copy-paste may be the first step, but leaving a note in that state is the failure; refactor it a little on almost every encounter, reframing and critiquing it. | Copy and paste may be the first step but the failure is to leave a note like this. | https://forum.zettelkasten.de/discussion/comment/11015#Comment_11015 | Will | 2021-03-15 | own-words,failure-mode | Vanilla API v2 2026-11-04 |
| 08-1665 | Keep a visible signal (e.g. title format) distinguishing notes primarily in someone else's words from your own syntheses; a cluster of linked literature notes signals that you should write your own evergreen synthesis. | I find value in being able to see at a glance from a link title whether it is something I primarily wrote or if it was primarily written by someone else. | https://forum.zettelkasten.de/discussion/comment/11052#Comment_11052 | davecan | 2021-03-17 | literature-note,own-words,title | Vanilla API v2 2026-11-04 |
| 08-1666 | Enter reading notes directly into the Zettelkasten, not a reference manager, so they can be refactored and edited in place; keep the marked-up PDFs in the reference manager. | Zotero is a great reference manager but a poor zettelkasten. Notes take while reading is best entered directly into your zettelkasten. | https://forum.zettelkasten.de/discussion/comment/12618#Comment_12618 | Will | 2021-08-17 | literature-note,tooling | Vanilla API v2 2026-11-04 |
| 08-1667 | Keep literature notes in a separate place, because small keyword notes scattered through the slip-box clutter and dilute it; the value is in the permanent notes made from them (citing Ahrens). | So by having them separate you are keeping your zettelkasten from getting diluted with crap. | https://forum.zettelkasten.de/discussion/comment/12619#Comment_12619 | Nick | 2021-08-17 | literature-note,failure-mode | Vanilla API v2 2026-11-04 |
| 08-1668 | A well-crafted summary of a text is itself a good zettel, since it is your idea of that text. | If your "summaries of texts read" are well crafted, they are good zettels. | https://forum.zettelkasten.de/discussion/comment/12622#Comment_12622 | Perikles | 2021-08-17 | literature-note,own-words | Vanilla API v2 2026-11-04 |
| 08-1669 | Literature notes summarize your reading, not the text: what you found and didn't find, how, and where. | They do not summarise the text, they summarise the read, i.e. what you found and haven't found, by reading it, how you found it, where you found it, etc. | https://forum.zettelkasten.de/discussion/comment/12623#Comment_12623 | zk_1000 | 2021-08-17 | literature-note | Vanilla API v2 2026-11-04 |
| 08-1670 | In exegetical fields like philosophy, ideas can't be divorced from their source, so separating literature notes from permanent notes is impractical; keep them together, with bibliographic detail in the reference manager. | For a fairly exegetical field like philosophy that mostly deals with the analysis of works and their constituent ideas rather than just general problems, the distinction seems impractical. | https://forum.zettelkasten.de/discussion/comment/12624#Comment_12624 | Privid | 2021-08-17 | literature-note,sources | Vanilla API v2 2026-11-04 |
| 08-1671 | Enter rough first-draft notes whole into the Zettelkasten and edit them in place into later versions; no separate collection of raw literature notes is needed. | I'm suggesting that they can be entered whole cloth without censoring or editing into the zettelkasten. | https://forum.zettelkasten.de/discussion/comment/12633#Comment_12633 | Will | 2021-08-19 | literature-note,evergreen | Vanilla API v2 2026-11-04 |
| 08-1672 | Rough reading notes stay outside the Zettelkasten as a separate file; so much distillation happens before the first zettels that including them is not useful. | I don't consider the "rough first draft" notes to be part of my ZK and they aren't entered as such. | https://forum.zettelkasten.de/discussion/comment/12632#Comment_12632 | GeoEng51 | 2021-08-18 | literature-note,process | Vanilla API v2 2026-11-04 |
| 08-1673 | Literature notes are written in the context of the book (idea X from page Y of book Z); permanent notes are written out of that context, for developing ideas in the Zettelkasten; keep literature notes as a per-book idea index in a separate folder. | Literature notes are written in the direct context of the book you are taking them from (e.g. X idea from Y page of Z book). | https://forum.zettelkasten.de/discussion/comment/12635#Comment_12635 | Nick | 2021-08-19 | literature-note | Vanilla API v2 2026-11-04 |
| 08-1674 | In history, mix verbatim copies of evidence and effective descriptions with paraphrases that give context for your comments, and keep all first-level notes permanently because conclusions must refer back to the evidence. | I copy verbatim evidence that I may want to use later on, or descriptions that I find particularly effective. | https://forum.zettelkasten.de/discussion/comment/12647#Comment_12647 | pgrhowarth | 2021-08-21 | sources,own-words,literature-note | Vanilla API v2 2026-11-04 |
| 08-1675 | When you meet a citation in your area of interest that you may look up or link to, create a citation note titled like "Jones 2007" with the reference (optionally a formatted citation) and link to it from any note that needs it. | So I create a note titled Jones 2007 and store it in a section of my ZK called "Citations". | https://forum.zettelkasten.de/discussion/comment/25563#Comment_25563 | tomp | 2026-07-01 | literature-note,sources | Vanilla API v2 2026-11-04 |
| 08-1676 | Don't put fleeting notes in the Zettelkasten: fleeting notes are meant to be discarded, permanent notes to be improved, so write notes with the intention of being permanent even if they start out bad. | Fleeting notes are meant to be discarded. | 08-1677 | Some scientific facts can't meaningfully be rephrased in your own words, because they are standard statements written the same way by many authors. | that note's idea is a fact and I don't see how I could it rephrase it in my own words. | https://forum.zettelkasten.de/discussion/comment/16717#Comment_16717 | Mirawen | 2022-11-05 | own-words,failure-mode | Vanilla API v2 2026-11-04 |
| 08-1678 | In science, write a reference note per paper with significant results plus your own thoughts and criticism, linked to related papers and zettels; such a paper summary can act as a zettel itself. | In a way, my summary of the paper acts as a zettel in and of itself. | https://forum.zettelkasten.de/discussion/comment/16740#Comment_16740 | prometheanhindsight | 2022-11-07 | literature-note,linking | Vanilla API v2 2026-11-04 |
| 08-1679 | When new to a topic, reading notes of others' statements are valuable building blocks: they build understanding, bookmark the source, aid recall, and supply citations; keep them as atomic notes rather than one note. | So in my view, these basic reading notes form important building blocks for everything that comes on top of it. | https://forum.zettelkasten.de/discussion/comment/16805#Comment_16805 | msteffens | 2022-11-16 | literature-note,atomicity,sources | Vanilla API v2 2026-11-04 |
| 08-1680 | Don't keep literature notes and permanent notes in separate apps: without duplicating text, each app's search would be impoverished. | I try not to duplicate verbiage in notes, so if I kept notes in separate apps, the searches in each would be impoverished. | https://forum.zettelkasten.de/discussion/comment/10497#Comment_10497 | Will | 2021-02-03 | literature-note,tooling | Vanilla API v2 2026-11-04 |
| 08-1681 | Keep literal quotes when you'll reuse them verbatim or care about how something was said; a turn of phrase can be worth holding for inspiration. | I'm also often interested in the poetry of how something was said. So I may hold on to a turn of phrase, or way of explaining something, that may be useful as inspiration. | https://forum.zettelkasten.de/discussion/comment/1949#Comment_1949 | micahredding | 2018-11-14 | sources,own-words | Vanilla API v2 2026-11-04 |
| 08-1682 | If a source's ideas are easily separable, split them into atomic notes immediately; if they are interconnected or complex, first write a longer note following the source's logic and split it later. | If ideas or concepts in a source are easily separable (e.g., in a well-written modern academic paper), sure, split them out into atomic notes immediately. | https://forum.zettelkasten.de/discussion/comment/16406#Comment_16406 | erikh | 2022-09-05 | literature-note,atomicity | Vanilla API v2 2026-11-04 |
| 08-1683 | Reading notes should be very short and selective, made about the content while reading (citing Ahrens, similar to Luhmann's practice). | He encourages being very short and selective. | 08-1684 | Put all the messy details (facts, quotes, keywords, bibliographic data) in big source notes so your few decontextualized atomic notes can stay neat and selective; link the source note for shared context. | Having reading notes makes it easier to keep the atomic notes neat. | https://forum.zettelkasten.de/discussion/comment/26484#Comment_26484 | harr | 2026-09-21 | literature-note,atomicity | Vanilla API v2 2026-11-04 |
| 08-1685 | The point of literature notes depends on your reading goals and strategy and on what kind of notes you want to have available later. | The point of literature notes depends very much on your reading goals and reading strategy. | https://forum.zettelkasten.de/discussion/comment/26481#Comment_26481 | harr | 2026-09-21 | literature-note,process | Vanilla API v2 2026-11-04 |
| 08-1686 | The most valuable part of a literature note is a 2-3 sentence summary at the top of why the source matters for your thinking, above the quotes and reading notes. | a 2-3 sentence summary of why the source is important for my thinking | https://forum.zettelkasten.de/discussion/comment/26497#Comment_26497 | mlbrandt | 2026-09-21 | literature-note,sources | Vanilla API v2 2026-11-04 |

| 08-1688 | A risk of literature notes is treating them as your main notes while neglecting the atomic notes you meant to write. | which was that I began treating them as my main notes while neglecting the atomic notes I was supposed to make. | https://forum.zettelkasten.de/discussion/comment/26542#Comment_26542 | libver | 2026-09-24 | literature-note,failure-mode | Vanilla API v2 2026-11-04 |

| 08-1690 | Prefer Allosso's terms: a "source note" holds a citation with or without annotations, and a "point note" should make a point, which gets at what a zettel is for without the detour through atomicity. | The term Point Note is a reminder that a Point Note (what we would call a Zettel) should make a point | https://forum.zettelkasten.de/discussion/comment/16370#Comment_16370 | ZettelDistraction | 2022-08-30 | literature-note,title | Vanilla API v2 2026-11-04 |
